73-11-05-700-801-F00Fuel Manifold - Inspection/Check ATA 73 · Task 700-801-F00

Task Description

2. Fuel Manifold - Inspection/Check (Figure 601)

A. References

Reference Title 24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201) 24-22-00-860-812 Remove Electrical Power (P/B 201) 26-11-02-400-802 Core Fire Detection Harness - Installation (P/B 401) 71-00-02-000-801-F00 Power Plant - Removal (P/B 401) 71-00-02-400-801-F00 Power Plant - Installation (P/B 401) 73-11-04-000-804-F02 Fuel Nozzle Removal (P/B 401) 73-11-04-000-805-F01 Fuel Nozzle Removal (P/B 401) 73-11-04-400-804-F02 Fuel Nozzle Installation (P/B 401) 73-11-04-400-805-F01 Fuel Nozzle Installation (P/B 401) 78-31-00-010-801-F00 Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201) 78-31-00-010-804-F00 Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201)

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 411 Engine 1 - Engine 421 Engine 2 - Engine

C. Procedure

SUBTASK 73-11-05-840-001-F00 WARNING DO THESE SPECIFIED TASKS IN THE CORRECT SEQUENCE BEFORE YOU OPEN THE THRUST REVERSERS: RETRACT THE LEADING EDGE, DO THE DEACTIVATION PROCEDURES FOR THE LEADING EDGE AND THE THRUST REVERSERS (FOR GROUND MAINTENANCE), AND OPEN THE FAN COWL PANELS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE ABOVE SEQUENCE, INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (1) Do this task: Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-801-F00. SUBTASK 73-11-05-900-001-F00 (2) If you find damage to the fuel manifold that is not in the limits, then replace the engine, unless you are given other instructions. NOTE: The fuel manifold is not a Line Replaceable Unit (LRU). To remove the fuel manifold, you must disassemble the engine. (a) These are the tasks: Power Plant - Removal, TASK 71-00-02-000-801-F00, Power Plant - Installation, TASK 71-00-02-400-801-F00. SUBTASK 73-11-05-210-001-F00 (3) Visually examine the fuel manifold tubes: 73-11-05 (a) Cracks, splits, or kinks in the manifold tubes are not acceptable. (b) Wrinkles in the bends of the manifold tubes are acceptable with this limit: 1) The depth of the wrinkle must be less than 3 percent of the outside diameter of the tube. (c) Dents in the manifold are acceptable with these limits: 1) Not more than one dent in one linear foot (0.305 M) of the tube. 2) The depth of the dent must be less than 20 percent of the outside diameter of the tube. 3) The minimum radius of the dent must be greater than 0.070 inch (1.78 mm). (d) Nicks, scratches, scores, and chafed areas are acceptable with this limit: 1) The depth of the nick, scratch, score, and chafed area must be less than 0.005 inch (0.13 mm) in depth after the tube is blended to a smooth radius. SUBTASK 73-11-05-210-002-F00 (4) Examine the fuel nozzle supply tubes: (a) Cracks, splits, or kinks in the nozzle supply tubes are not acceptable. (b) Wrinkles in the bends of the nozzle supply tubes are acceptable with these limits: 1) The depth of the wrinkle must be less than 3 percent of the outside diameter of the tube. (c) Dents in the nozzle supply tubes are acceptable with these limits: 1) Not more than one dent in each of the tubes. 2) The depth of the dent must be less than 20 percent of the outside diameter of the tube. 3) The minimum radius of the dent must be greater than 0.070 inch (1.78 mm). (d) Flattened areas in the nozzle supply tubes are acceptable with these limits: 1) The length of the flattened area must be less than 2 times the outside diameter of the tube. 2) The flattened area must change the contour of the tube by less than 10 percent of its outside diameter. (e) Nicks, scratches, scores, and chafed areas are acceptable with this limit: 1) The depth of the nick, scratch, score, and chafed area must be less than 0.005 inch (0.13 mm) in depth after the tube is blended to a smooth radius. RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH SINGLE ANNULAR COMBUSTOR (SAC) ENGINES 2) For fuel nozzle supply tube number 7, the maximum limit of depth for nick, scratch, score, and chafed area is 0.018 inch (0.45 mm) after removal of high metal. a) Make sure that there is minimum clearance of 0.125 inch (3.17 mm) with the bolt of the fire detector harness bracket (TASK 26-11-02-400-802). SUBTASK 73-11-05-210-004-F00 NOTE: Do this inspection if the fuel manifold shroud coupling nut has been disconnected from its mating surface. (5) Visually examine the threads of the fuel manifold shroud coupling nut for damage. (a) Damage must be less than one-half of a thread, cumulative or continuous. 73-11-05 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH SINGLE ANNULAR COMBUSTOR (SAC) ENGINES (Continued) SUBTASK 73-11-05-900-005-F00 (6) If you find damage to the shroud coupling nut thread that is not in limits, then replace the shroud coupling nut. These are the steps: (a) Remove the fuel nozzle that mates with the damaged shroud coupling nut. Do this task, Fuel Nozzle Removal, TASK 73-11-04-000-805-F01 or Fuel Nozzle Removal, TASK 73-11-04-000-804-F02. (b) With applicable tool, remove the retaining ring from the shroud. (c) Slide the shroud aft and remove it from the fuel manifold tube. (d) Slide the shroud coupling nut off the shroud. If the shroud coupling nut does not remove easily from the shroud, replace the shroud coupling nut and the shroud. (e) Install the shroud coupling nut on the shroud. Make sure that the open portion of the nut will face aft when the shroud is installed on the fuel manifold tube. (f) Install the shroud on the fuel manifold tube. Make sure that the open portion of the shroud faces aft after installation. (g) With the applicable tool, install the retaining ring in the shroud. After installation, apply pressure to the retaining ring to make sure it is retained in the groove in the shroud. (h) Install the fuel nozzle, do this task Fuel Nozzle Installation, TASK 73-11-04-400-805-F01 or Fuel Nozzle Installation, TASK 73-11-04-400-804-F02. RYR ALL SUBTASK 73-11-05-900-003-F00 (7) If you find damage to the fuel manifold that is not in the limits, then replace the engine, unless you are given other instructions. NOTE: The fuel manifold is not a Line Replaceable Unit (LRU). To remove the fuel manifold, you must disassemble the engine. (a) These are the tasks: Power Plant - Removal, TASK 71-00-02-000-801-F00, Power Plant - Installation, TASK 71-00-02-400-801-F00. SUBTASK 73-11-05-900-002-F00 (8) Do these steps to prepare to examine the nipples and ferrules: NOTE: Do this portion of the fuel manifold inspection if you think you have a leak or if you find damage after you removed a fuel nozzle. (a) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. 1) Make sure that the BAT switch on panel P5-13 is set to OFF and install a DO-NOT-OPERATE tag. SUBTASK 73-11-05-210-003-F00 (9) Examine the mating surfaces of the nipples and ferrules: (a) If not already done, disconnect the fuel supply tube from the applicable fuel nozzle (TASK 73-11-04-000-805-F01 or TASK 73-11-04-000-804-F02). (b) Axial scratches in Areas A and C are acceptable after the surface is blended smooth. (c) Axial scratches in Area B are not acceptable. (d) Circumferential scratches in Areas A and C are acceptable after the surface is blended smooth. 73-11-05 (e) Circumferential scratches in Area B are acceptable with these limits: 1) The surface is blended smooth. 2) The scratch is too small to measure with a 0.040 inch (1.02 mm) radius ball scriber. (f) Nicks and pits in Areas A, B, and C are not acceptable. (g) Damaged threads are acceptable with these limits: 1) Use a tap or a die to chase the damaged threads. 2) After you chase the threads, the damage must be less than one-half of a thread, cumulative or continuous. (h) Connect the fuel supply tube to the applicable fuel nozzle (TASK 73-11-04-400-805-F01 or TASK 73-11-04-400-804-F02). SUBTASK 73-11-05-900-004-F00 (10) If you find damage to the fuel nozzle that is not in the limits, then replace the Fuel Nozzle. (a) These are the tasks: Fuel Nozzle Removal, TASK 73-11-04-000-805-F01 or Fuel Nozzle Removal, TASK 73-11-04-000-804-F02, Fuel Nozzle Installation, TASK 73-11-04-400-805-F01 or Fuel Nozzle Installation, TASK 73-11-04-400-804-F02. SUBTASK 73-11-05-840-002-F00 (11) Put the airplane in a serviceable condition: (a) If you want to supply electrical power, do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811. 1) Remove the DO-NOT-OPERATE tag from the BAT switch on panel P5-13. WARNING OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS IN THE PROCEDURE TO CLOSE THE THRUST REVERSER. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS, INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (b) Do this task: Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-804-F00.

73-11-06-000-801-F00IDG Oil Cooler Removal ATA 73 · Task 000-801-F00

Task Description

2. IDG Oil Cooler Removal (Figure 401)

A. General

(1) The engine has one IDG oil cooler. (2) The IDG oil cooler is on the fan frame, aft of the lubrication unit, at the 7:00 o'clock position.

B. References

Reference Title 24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201) 24-22-00-860-812 Remove Electrical Power (P/B 201) 70-30-01-910-802-F00 Seals (Preformed Packings and O-Rings) and Gaskets (P/B 201) 71-11-02-010-801-F00 Open the Fan Cowl Panels (P/B 201)

Reference Description STD-858 Tag - DO NOT OPERATE STD-1054 Container - Fuel Resistant, 5-Gallon (19-Liter)

Zone Area 411 Engine 1 - Engine 421 Engine 2 - Engine

E. Prepare for the Removal

SUBTASK 73-11-06-840-001-F00 (1) Prepare for the procedure as follows: (a) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811. (b) Make sure that the engine start lever is in the CUTOFF position. 1) Install a DO NOT OPERATE tag, STD-858, on the applicable engine start lever. (c) Make sure that the ENG VALVE CLOSED (engine fuel shutoff valve) light on the fuel control panel (P5 overhead panel) is dim. NOTE: The light for the engine fuel shutoff valve has three positions: 1) bright when the valve is in transition or does not agree with the commanded position; 2) dim when the valve is closed; or 3) off when the valve is opened. 73-11-06 (d) Make sure that the SPAR VALVE CLOSED light on the fuel control panel (P5 overhead panel) is dim. NOTE: The light for the spar shutoff valve has three positions: 1) bright when the valve is in transition or does not agree with the commanded position; 2) dim when the valve is closed; or 3) off when the valve is opened. (e) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. 1) Make sure that the BAT switch on P5-13 panel is set to OFF and install a DO NOT OPERATE tag, STD-858. (f) Do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00. F. IDG Oil Cooler Removal SUBTASK 73-11-06-020-002-F00 (1) Open the 1/4-turn Omega clamps [10]. (a) Remove the J5, J6, J7, and J8 wire harnesses from the Omega clamps [10]. (b) Move the wire harnesses away from the IDG oil cooler [5]. 1) If it is necessary, use string or tape to hold the wires out of your way. SUBTASK 73-11-06-680-001-F00 WARNING DO NOT GET FUEL IN YOUR MOUTH, OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE FUEL. KEEP THE FUEL AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . FUEL IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE, WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE COMPONENTS OF THE OIL SYSTEM IF THE ENGINE IS HOT . THESE COMPONENTS STAY HOTTER THAN OTHER COMPONENTS. HOT COMPONENTS CAN BURN YOU. WARNING DO NOT OPEN THE OIL SYSTEM UNTIL THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO. THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO APPROXIMATELY 5 MINUTES AFTER AN ENGINE IS STOPPED. A PRESSURIZED OIL SYSTEM CAN RELEASE A SPRAY OF HOT OIL THAT CAN BURN YOU. WARNING DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON YOU. PUT ON CLOTHES, GOGGLES, AND OTHER EQUIPMENT FOR PROTECTION, OR LET THE ENGINE BECOME COOL. HOT OIL CAN BURN YOU. CAUTION DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON THE ENGINE OR OTHER COMPONENTS. IMMEDIATELY CLEAN THE COMPONENT IF OIL FALLS ON IT . OIL CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO PAINT AND RUBBER. (2) Drain the IDG oil cooler [5] as follows: (a) Put a 5-gallon (19-liter) fuel resistant container, STD-1054, under the IDG oil cooler [5]. (b) Remove the drain plug [20]. 1) Let the oil drain into the container. (c) Remove and discard the O-ring [15] from the drain plug [20]. (d) Keep the drain plug [20] for the subsequent installation. 73-11-06 SUBTASK 73-11-06-020-003-F00 (3) Remove the bracket [25] as follows: (a) Remove the bolt [40] and clamp [35] that attach the oil-out tube [30] to the bracket [25]. (b) Remove the bolts [50] that attach the bracket [25] to the IDG oil cooler [5]. 1) Remove the bracket [25] from the IDG oil cooler [5]. (c) Keep the container under the IDG oil cooler [5], to catch the unwanted fuel and oil that flows during the removal procedure. SUBTASK 73-11-06-020-004-F00 (4) Disconnect the fuel-in tube [60] from the IDG oil cooler [5] as follows: (a) Remove the bolts [70] that attach the fuel-in tube [60] and the gasket [65] to the IDG oil cooler [5]. (b) Move the fuel-in tube [60] out of the way, to make sure that it is not damaged. 1) If it is necessary, use lockwire or tape to keep the tube out of the way. 2) If the gasket [65] is serviceable, then keep it with the tube for the subsequent installation. NOTE: Refer to this task (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00) for gasket and seal maintenance practices. (c) Install protective covers on the fuel-in tube [60] and the IDG oil cooler [5]. SUBTASK 73-11-06-020-005-F00 (5) Disconnect the fuel-out tube [80] from the IDG oil cooler [5] as follows: (a) Remove the bolts [75] that attach the fuel-out tube [80] and the gasket [85] to the IDG oil cooler [5]. NOTE: Refer to this task (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00) for gasket and seal maintenance practices. (c) Install protective covers on the fuel-out tube [80] and the IDG oil cooler [5]. (6) Disconnect the oil tube [30] and tube [90] from the IDG oil cooler [5] as follows: (a) Use two wrenches to disconnect the oil-out tube [30] from the IDG oil cooler [5]. E. Prepare for the Installation

SUBTASK 73-11-06-840-002-F00 (1) Prepare for the procedure as follows: (a) Remove the protective covers from the Integrated Drive Generator (IDG) oil cooler [5] and its oil and fuel tubes. (b) Make sure that the mating surfaces of the IDG oil cooler [5] are clean. (c) Examine all of the component mating surfaces and the adjacent areas. 1) Make sure that the mating surfaces are clean. 2) Make sure that the components are not damaged. (d) Re-install the protective covers on the IDG oil cooler [5] and its oil and fuel tubes. SUBTASK 73-11-06-420-002-F00 (2) Install the drain plug [20] into the IDG oil cooler [5]: WARNING DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN. (a) Lubricate the new O-ring [15] with oil, D00623 [CP5066]. (b) Install the new O-ring [15] on the drain plug [20]. (c) Lubricate the threads of the drain plug [20] with oil, D00623 [CP5066]. (d) Install the drain plug [20]. 1) Tighten the drain plug [20] to a torque of 45 in-lb (5.08 N·m) - 55 in-lb (6.21 N·m). (e) Install the safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the drain plug [20]. 73-11-06 SUBTASK 73-11-06-020-008-F00 (3) Install the oil-out nipple [120] and the oil-in nipple [135] on the IDG oil cooler [5]: (a) Remove the protective covers from the oil-out nipple [120], the oil-in nipple [135], and the oil ports on the IDG oil cooler [5]. (b) Lubricate the threads of bolts [130] and bolts [145] with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101]. (c) Put the oil-out nipple [120] in the correct position on the IDG oil cooler [5]. 1) Install the bolts [130] to attach the oil-out nipple [120] to the IDG oil cooler [5]. 2) Tighten the bolts [130] to 49 in-lb (5.54 N·m) - 53 in-lb (5.99 N·m). 73-11-07-000-801-F00Servo Fuel Heater Removal ATA 73 · Task 000-801-F00

Task Description

2. Servo Fuel Heater Removal (Figure 401)

A. General

(1) The servo fuel heater is on the engine, below the left fan cowl panel, at the 8:00 o'clock position. (2) The servo fuel heater is installed on the top of the fuel pump package, adjacent to the main oil/fuel heat exchanger. (3) The servo fuel heater is attached to the Hydro-Mechanical Unit (HMU) and main oil/fuel heat exchanger.

B. References

G. Prepare for the Removal

SUBTASK 73-11-07-840-001-F00 (1) Do these steps to prepare the airplane for the removal: (a) Make sure that the applicable start lever is in the CUTOFF position. 1) Install a DO NOT OPERATE tag, STD-858, on the applicable start lever. 73-11-07 (b) Make sure that the ENG VALVE CLOSED (engine fuel shutoff valve) light, on the P5-2 fuel control panel, is dim. NOTE: The light for the engine fuel shutoff valve has three positions: - Bright when the valve is in transition or does not agree with the commanded position - Dim when the valve is closed - Off when the valve is opened. (c) Make sure that the SPAR VALVE CLOSED light, on the P5-2 fuel control panel, is dim. NOTE: The light for the spar shutoff valve has three positions: - Bright when the valve is in transition or does not agree with the commanded position - Dim when the valve is closed - Off when the valve is opened. (d) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. 1) Make sure that the BAT switch, on the P5-13 panel, is set to the OFF position. a) Install a DO NOT OPERATE tag, STD-858, on the BAT switch. (e) Do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00.

H. Servo Fuel Heater Removal

SUBTASK 73-11-07-680-001-F00 (1) Do these steps to drain the fuel from the fuel pump [5]: WARNING DO NOT GET FUEL IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE FUEL. PUT ON GOGGLES, AND GLOVES WHEN YOU USE FUEL. KEEP FUEL AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . FUEL IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. FUEL CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (a) Put a 5-gallon (19-liter) fuel resistant container, STD-1054, below the fuel pump. (b) Remove the safety wire from the drain plug [3]. (c) Remove the drain plug [3] from the fuel filter cover. (d) Let the fuel drain in the container. (e) Remove and discard the O-ring [4] from the drain plug [3]. WARNING DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN. (f) Lubricate a new O-ring [4] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or vaseline, D00672 [CP5070]. (g) Install a O-ring [4] on the drain plug [3]. (h) Lubricate the threads of the drain plug [3] with oil, D00623 [CP5066]. (i) Install the drain plug [3]. 1) Tighten the drain plug [3] to 45 in-lb (5.1 N·m) - 55 in-lb (6.2 N·m). (j) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the drain plug [3] (TASK 70-20-01-800-805-F00). 73-11-07 SUBTASK 73-11-07-210-001-F00 (2) Make sure that the container stays below the fuel pump during the removal procedure. (a) As you disassemble the servo-fuel heater [1], let the unwanted fuel and oil drain into the container. SUBTASK 73-11-07-020-001-F00 WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE COMPONENTS OF THE OIL SYSTEM IF THE ENGINE IS HOT . THESE COMPONENTS STAY HOTTER THAN OTHER COMPONENTS. HOT COMPONENTS CAN BURN YOU. WARNING DO NOT OPEN THE OIL SYSTEM UNTIL THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO. THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO APPROXIMATELY 5 MINUTES AFTER AN ENGINE IS STOPPED. A PRESSURIZED OIL SYSTEM CAN RELEASE A SPRAY OF HOT OIL THAT CAN BURN YOU. WARNING DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON YOU. PUT ON CLOTHES, GOGGLES, AND OTHER EQUIPMENT FOR PROTECTION, OR LET THE ENGINE BECOME COOL. HOT OIL CAN BURN YOU. WARNING DO NOT GET ENGINE OIL ON YOUR SKIN FOR A LONG TIME. FLUSH THE OIL FROM YOUR SKIN WITH WATER. THE OIL IS POISONOUS AND CAN GO THROUGH YOUR SKIN AND IN TO YOUR BODY . CAUTION DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON THE ENGINE OR OTHER COMPONENTS. IMMEDIATELY CLEAN THE COMPONENT IF OIL FALLS ON IT . OIL CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO PAINT AND RUBBER. CAUTION USE TWO WRENCHES TO LOOSEN THE COUPLING NUT . USE ONE TO HOLD THE FITTING, AND THE OTHER TO LOOSEN THE COUPLING NUT . IF YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT . (3) Do these steps to remove the oil tube [11] from the servo-fuel heater [1]: (a) Remove the nut [15], bolt [16], and clamps [17] that hold the oil tube [11] on the bracket. NOTE: The nut and bolt also hold a clamp for the fuel tube. 1) Let the other clamp stay on the fuel tube [14]. NOTE: The clamp can be removed from the fuel tube and kept for installation. (b) Remove the nut [18], bolt [19], and clamp [20] that hold the oil tube [11] on the bracket. (c) Use two wrenches to disconnect the oil tube [11] from the oil tube [10]. (d) Remove the four bolts [13] that hold the oil tube [11] and gasket [12] on the servo-fuel heater [1]. 1) If the gasket [12] is serviceable, keep it with the tube for the subsequent installation (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00). 2) Remove the oil tube [11]. 73-11-07 a) Install protective covers on the oil tube [11], oil tube [10], and servo-fuel heater [1]. (b) Use two wrenches to disconnect the fuel tube [21] from the fuel tube [22]. E. Servo Fuel Heater Installation

SUBTASK 73-11-07-020-005-F00 (1) Install the servo-fuel heater [1] on the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40]. (a) Remove the protective covers from the mating surfaces of the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40] and the servo-fuel heater [1]. (b) Lubricate the applicable parts: WARNING DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN. 1) Lubricate the gasket [41] with oil, D00623 [CP5066]. 2) Lubricate the threads of the studs on the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40] with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101]. (c) Put the gasket [41], servo-fuel heater [1], and the bracket [44] in the correct position on the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40]. (d) Install the three nuts [42] that hold the servo-fuel heater [1] and the bracket [44] on the outboard side of the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40]. (e) Install the three nuts [42] and the three washers [43] that hold the servo-fuel heater [1] on the inboard side of the main oil/fuel heat exchanger [40]. 1) Tighten the six nuts [42] to 98 in-lb (11 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12 N·m) in a criss-cross pattern. (f) Install the nut [45], bolt [46], and the clamp [47] that hold the fuel tube [14] on the bracket [44]. 1) Tighten the nut [45] to 98 in-lb (11 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12 N·m). SUBTASK 73-11-07-420-001-F00 (2) Do these steps to install the nipple [48] and nipple [50]: 73-11-07 WARNING DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN. (a) Lubricate two new O-rings [49] with oil, D00623 [CP5066]. (b) Install one O-ring [49] in the groove of nipple [48] and nipple [50]. (c) Lubricate the threads of the nipple [48] and nipple [50] with oil, D00623 [CP5066]. (d) Install the nipple [48] and nipple [50] in the servo fuel-heater ports. (e) Tighten the nipple [48] and nipple [50] to 361 in-lb (41 N·m) - 399 in-lb (45 N·m). (f) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], between the nipple [48] and nipple [50]. 73-11-08-000-801-F00Burner Staging Valve Removal ATA 73 · Task 000-801-F00

Task Description

2. Burner Staging Valve Removal (Figure 401)

A. General

(1) The burner staging valve is found on the lower side of engine, forward of the fuel nozzles. (2) For this procedure the burner staging valve will be referred to as the BSV. RYR ALL POST SB CFM56-7B-73-054 AND POST SB CFM56-7B 73-044 (3) Engines with CFMI SB 73-044 do not have the Burner Staging Valve installed. RYR ALL POST SB CFM56-7B-73-054 (4) Engines with CFMI SB 73-054 do not have the Burner Staging Valve installed. The Burner Staging Valve is replaced by a fuel cover. RYR ALL

B. References

Reference Title 24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201) 24-22-00-860-812 Remove Electrical Power (P/B 201) 70-30-01-910-802-F00 Seals (Preformed Packings and O-Rings) and Gaskets (P/B 201) 78-31-00-010-801-F00 Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201)

C. Tools/Equipment

Reference Description STD-1054 Container - Fuel Resistant, 5-Gallon (19-Liter)

D. Location Zones

Zone Area 411 Engine 1 - Engine 421 Engine 2 - Engine

E. Prepare for the Removal

SUBTASK 73-11-08-840-001-F00 (1) Do these steps to prepare for the procedure: (a) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811. (b) Make sure the engine start lever is in the CUTOFF position. 1) Install a DO-NOT-OPERATE tag on the applicable engine start lever. 73-11-08 (c) Make sure the ENG VALVE CLOSED (engine fuel shutoff valve) light on the fuel control panel (P5 overhead panel) is dim. NOTE: The light for the engine fuel shutoff valve has three positions: 1) bright when the valve is in transition or does not agree with the commanded position; 2) dim when the valve is closed; or 3) off when the valve is opened. (d) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. 1) Make sure that the BAT switch on panel P5-13 is set to OFF and install a DO-NOT-OPERATE tag. WARNING DO THESE SPECIFIED TASKS IN THE CORRECT SEQUENCE BEFORE YOU OPEN THE THRUST REVERSER: RETRACT THE LEADING EDGE, DO THE DEACTIVATION OF THE LEADING EDGE AND THE THRUST REVERSER (FOR GROUND MAINTENANCE), AND OPEN THE FAN COWL PANELS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THIS ABOVE SEQUENCE, INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (e) Do this task: Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-801-F00. F. Burner Staging Valve Removal (BSV) SUBTASK 73-11-08-020-001-F00 (1) Do these steps to remove the BSV [4] from the engine: (a) Disconnect the electrical connectors DP0906 [1] and DP1006 [5] from the BSV [4]. (b) Put a 5-gallon (19-liter) fuel resistant container, STD-1054below the BSV [4]. WARNING DO NOT GET FUEL IN YOUR MOUTH, OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE FUEL. KEEP THE FUEL AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . FUEL IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE, WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (c) Remove the four bolts [3] that hold the burner staging valve [4] to the fuel supply manifold. (d) Remove the BSV [4]. (e) Remove the gasket [2]. (f) Examine the gasket [2] (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00). 1) If the gasket [2] is damaged, discard the gasket. 2) If there is no damage, keep the gasket [2] for the subsequent installation. (g) Install protective covers on the BSV [4] openings and the fuel supply manifold.

H. Fuel Supply Hose Removal

SUBTASK 73-11-10-680-001-F00 (1) Do these steps to drain the fuel from the fuel system: WARNING DO NOT GET FUEL IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE FUEL. PUT ON GOGGLES AND GLOVES WHEN YOU DO FUEL SYSTEM MAINTENANCE. KEEP FUEL AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . FUEL IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. FUEL CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (a) Put a 5-gallon (19-liter) fuel resistant container, STD-1054, below the fuel pump. 73-11-10 (b) Remove the drain plug [20] from the fuel filter cover. (c) Let the fuel drain in the container. (d) Remove and discard the packing [21] from the drain plug. (e) Keep the container below the engine to catch the unwanted fuel that flows. WARNING DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN. (f) Lubricate a new packing [21] with oil, D00623 [CP5066]. (g) Install the new packing [21] on the drain plug [20]. (h) Lubricate the threads of the drain plug [20] with oil, D00623 [CP5066]. (i) Install the drain plug [20]. 1) Tighten the drain plug [20] to 45 in-lb (5.1 N·m) - 55 in-lb (6.2 N·m). (j) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the drain plug [20]. SUBTASK 73-11-10-020-001-F00 (2) Remove the bolts [3] and strap clamps [2] that hold the fuel supply hose [1] to the fan case. (a) Keep the bolts [3] and strap clamps [2] for the subsequent installation. SUBTASK 73-11-10-020-002-F00 (3) Do these steps to disconnect the fuel supply hose [1] from the engine fuel tube: (a) Make sure that the container (5 gal.) is in a position to catch the remaining fuel that can flow from the fuel supply hose [1]. (b) Remove the bolts [5] that hold the fuel supply hose [1] and the gasket [6] to the engine fuel tube. 1) If the gasket [6] is serviceable, keep it with the tube for the subsequent installation (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00). 2) Keep the bolts [5] for the subsequent installation. SUBTASK 73-11-10-020-004-F00 (4) Do these steps to disconnect the fuel supply hose [1] from the strut fitting: (a) Use two wrenches to disconnect the B-nut from the strut fitting. NOTE: One wrench to hold the fuel supply hose and one wrench to turn the B-nut.

Installation Test SUBTASK 73-11-10-790-001-F00 (1) Do these steps to prepare for the installation test: (a) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811. 1) Remove the DO-NOT-OPERATE tag from the BAT switch on panel P5-13. (b) For engine 1, do this step: Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00458 ENGINE 1 IGNITION RIGHT A 3 C00153 ENGINE 1 IGNITION LEFT (c) For engine 2, do this step: Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name D 4 C00459 ENGINE 2 IGNITION RIGHT D 6 C00151 ENGINE 2 IGNITION LEFT (d) Do these steps to apply the boost pump pressure to the fuel pump inlet of the applicable engine: 1) Make sure that the two Engine Start Switches, on the P5 overhead panel, are in the OFF position. a) Attach DO NOT OPERATE tags, STD-858, to the two Engine Start Switches. 2) For engine 1, do this step: Make sure that this circuit breaker is closed: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name B 4 C00359 FUEL SPAR VALVE ENG 1 3) For engine 2, do this step: Make sure that this circuit breaker is closed: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name B 3 C00360 FUEL SPAR VALVE ENG 2 4) Move the applicable start lever to the IDLE position. 5) Make sure that the applicable SPAR VALVE CLOSED light, on the P5 overhead panel, comes on bright (valve in transition or when the valve does not agree with the commanded position) and then goes off (valve open). a) The applicable ENG VALVE CLOSED light will change to bright and not go off because engine fuel pump pressure (N2 turns) is necessary to open the valve. 6) For engine 1, do this step: 73-11-10 Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name B 4 C00359 FUEL SPAR VALVE ENG 1 7) For engine 2, do this step: Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name B 3 C00360 FUEL SPAR VALVE ENG 2 8) Move the applicable start lever to the CUTOFF position. (e) For engine 1, do this step: Put these switches, on the P5 overhead panel, to the ON position: 1) FUEL PUMP 1 FWD 2) FUEL PUMP 1 AFT . (f) For engine 2, do this step: Put these switches, on the P5 overhead panel, to the ON position: 1) FUEL PUMP 2 FWD 2) FUEL PUMP 2 AFT . SUBTASK 73-11-10-210-001-F00 (2) Examine the fittings on the fuel supply hose [1] for leaks. (a) If you find a leak, then repair the fitting as it is necessary. (b) If you do not find a leak, then continue. SUBTASK 73-11-10-840-003-F00 (3) Do these steps to put the airplane in a serviceable condition: (a) For engine 1, do this step: Put these switches, on the P5 overhead panel, to the OFF position: 1) FUEL PUMP 1 FWD 2) FUEL PUMP 1 AFT . (b) For engine 2, do this step: Put these switches, on the P5 overhead panel, to the OFF position: 1) FUEL PUMP 2 FWD 2) FUEL PUMP 2 AFT . (c) For engine 1, do this step: Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00458 ENGINE 1 IGNITION RIGHT A 3 C00153 ENGINE 1 IGNITION LEFT 73-11-10 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name B 4 C00359 FUEL SPAR VALVE ENG 1 (d) For engine 2, do this step: Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name D 4 C00459 ENGINE 2 IGNITION RIGHT D 6 C00151 ENGINE 2 IGNITION LEFT F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name B 3 C00360 FUEL SPAR VALVE ENG 2 (e) Remove the DO-NOT-OPERATE tags from the two Engine Start Switches. (f) Do this step: Close the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-410-801-F00. SUBTASK 73-11-10-720-002-F00 (4) Do the tests that are listed in the Power Plant Test Reference Table (TASK 71-00-00-800-811-F00).

73-11-11-000-801-F00Fuel Pump Removal ATA 73 · Task 000-801-F00

Task Description

2. Fuel Pump Removal (Figure 401, Figure 402, Figure 403, and Figure 404).

A. General

(1) This task gives the instructions on how to remove the fuel pump. (2) The fuel pump is on the engine at the 8:00 o'clock position below the left fan cowl panel. (3) The fuel pump package is made up of the fuel pump, Hydro-Mechanical Unit (HMU), fuel filter, main oil/fuel heat exchanger, servo fuel heater, and fuel filter differential pressure switch. (4) It is easier to complete the task if you remove and install the same size tube connectors at the same time.

B. References

Reference Title 24-22-00-860-812 Remove Electrical Power (P/B 201) 70-30-01-910-802-F00 Seals (Preformed Packings and O-Rings) and Gaskets (P/B 201) 71-11-02-010-801-F00 Open the Fan Cowl Panels (P/B 201) 72-63-01-000-801-F00 Handcranking Drive Cover Removal (P/B 201) 73-11-01-600-801-F00 Fuel Pump Preservation (P/B 201) 73-11-07-000-801-F00 Servo Fuel Heater Removal (P/B 401) 73-21-10-000-801-F00 HMU Removal (P/B 401) 73-34-01-000-801-F00 Fuel Filter Differential Pressure Switch or Fuel Filter Clogging Indicator Removal (P/B 401) 79-21-02-000-801-F00 Main Oil/Fuel Heat Exchanger Removal (P/B 401)

C. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description SPL-2189 Block - Jacking, Fuel Pump, MEC/HMU Part #: 856A2609G01 Supplier: 58828 STD-858 Tag - DO NOT OPERATE STD-1154 Container - 5 Gallon (19 Liter)

G. Prepare for the Removal

SUBTASK 73-11-11-840-003-F00 (1) Do these steps to prepare the airplane for the removal: (a) Make sure that the start levers are in CUTOFF position. 1) Install the DO NOT OPERATE tags, STD-858, on the start levers (b) Make sure that the ENG VALVE CLOSED (engine fuel shutoff valve) light, on the P5 overhead panel, is dim. NOTE: The light for the engine fuel shutoff valve has three positions: 1) bright when the valve is in transition or does not agree with the commanded position; 2) dim when the valve is closed; or 3) off when the valve is opened. (c) Make sure that the SPAR VALVE CLOSED (fuel spar shutoff valve) light, on the P5 overhead panel, is dim. 73-11-11 (d) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. NOTE: The removal of the electrical power is necessary before you disconnect the electrical and fluid connectors. You can reapply electrical power to the airplane after all of the electrical and fluid connectors are disconnected and the protective covers are installed. (e) Make sure that the BAT switch, on the P5-13 panel, is in the OFF position. 1) Install the DO NOT OPERATE tag, STD-858, on the BAT switch. SUBTASK 73-11-11-010-001-F00 (2) Do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00.

H. Fuel Pump Removal

SUBTASK 73-11-11-680-001-F00 (1) Do these steps to drain the fuel from the fuel pump [1]. WARNING DO NOT GET FUEL IN YOUR MOUTH, OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE FUEL. KEEP THE FUEL AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . FUEL IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE, WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (a) Put a 5 gallon (19 liter) container, STD-1154, below the fuel pump [1]. (b) Cut and discard safety wire from the drain plug [2]. (c) Remove the drain plug [2] from the fuel filter cover. (d) Let the fuel drain in the container. (e) Remove and discard the O-ring [3] from the drain plug [2]. WARNING DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN. (f) Lubricate a new O-ring [3] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or vaseline, D00672 [CP5070]. (g) Install the new O-ring [3] on the drain plug [2]. (h) Lubricate the threads of the drain plug [2] with oil, D00623 [CP5066]. (i) Install the drain plug [2]. 1) Tighten the drain plug [2] to 45.0 in-lb (5.08 N·m) - 55.0 in-lb (6.21 N·m). (j) Install the safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the drain plug [2]. SUBTASK 73-11-11-680-002-F00 (2) Make sure that the container stays below the fuel pump during the removal. (a) While the fuel pump assembly is disassembled, let the unwanted fuel and oil drain into the container. SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-001-F00 (3) Do this task: HMU Removal, TASK 73-21-10-000-801-F00. SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-002-F00 (4) Do this task: Handcranking Drive Cover Removal, TASK 72-63-01-000-801-F00. 73-11-11 SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-003-F00 (5) Do this task: Servo Fuel Heater Removal, TASK 73-11-07-000-801-F00. SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-004-F00 (6) Do this task: Main Oil/Fuel Heat Exchanger Removal, TASK 79-21-02-000-801-F00. SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-005-F00 CAUTION USE TWO WRENCHES TO LOOSEN THE TUBE COUPLING NUTS. USE ONE WRENCH TO HOLD THE NIPPLE FITTING. USE THE OTHER WRENCH TO LOOSEN THE COUPLING NUT . IF THE NIPPLE FITTING TURNS, DAMAGE CAN OCCUR. (7) Do these steps to disconnect the fuel tube [8] from the hose [4]: (a) Use two wrenches to disconnect the fuel tube [8] from the hose [4]. 1) If it is necessary, use lockwire or tape to keep the hose [4] out of the way. (b) Remove the fuel tube [8]. (c) Install protective covers on the fuel tube [8] and hose [4]. SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-006-F00 CAUTION USE TWO WRENCHES TO LOOSEN THE TUBE COUPLING NUTS. USE ONE WRENCH TO HOLD THE NIPPLE FITTING. USE THE OTHER WRENCH TO LOOSEN THE COUPLING NUT . IF THE NIPPLE FITTING TURNS, DAMAGE CAN OCCUR. (8) Do these steps to remove the fuel tube [16] from the hose [15] and from the fuel pump [1]: (a) Use two wrenches to disconnect the fuel tube [16] from the hose [15]. (b) Remove the nut [19], bolt [20], and clamp [21] that hold the fuel tube [16] to the bottom of the fuel pump [1]. (c) Remove the four bolts [18] that hold the fuel tube [16] and the gasket [17] to the fuel pump [1]. (d) Remove the fuel tube [16]. 1) If the gasket [17] is serviceable, then keep it with the tube for the subsequent installation (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00). (e) Install the protective covers on the fuel tube [16], hose [15], and fuel pump [1]. SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-007-F00 (9) Do these steps to disconnect the fuel supply hose [9] from the fuel pump inlet tube [12]: (a) Remove the four bolts [11] to disconnect the fuel supply hose [9] and the gasket [10] from the fuel pump inlet tube [12]. (b) Move the fuel supply hose [9] away from the fuel pump inlet tube [12]. NOTE: If you have difficulty when you try to move the fuel supply hose, then remove the bolts and clamps that hold the fuel supply hose to the fan case. 1) If the gasket [10] is serviceable, then keep it with the tube for the subsequent installation (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00). 2) Install the protective covers on the fuel supply hose [9] and the fuel pump inlet tube [12]. SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-008-F00 (10) Do these steps to remove the fuel pump [1]: 73-11-11 (a) Make sure that the protective covers are installed on all openings in the fuel pump [1]. (b) Remove the QAD bolt [22] and retaining packing [28]. 1) Install it in the opposite position with the jacking block, SPL-2189. a) Keep the ball seat washer [23] for the subsequent installation. WARNING BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU REMOVE THE FUEL PUMP . THE FUEL PUMP WEIGHS APPROXIMATELY 46.3 POUNDS (21 KG). THE WEIGHT CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT . CAUTION DO NOT LIFT THE FUEL PUMP BY THE DRIVE SHAFT . DO NOT LET THE DRIVE SHAFT SUPPORT THE WEIGHT OF THE FUEL PUMP . IF THE DRIVE SHAFT SUPPORTS THE WEIGHT OF THE FUEL PUMP , IT CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE FUEL PUMP SEALS. CAUTION YOU MUST HOLD THE WEIGHT OF THE FUEL PUMP AS YOU LOOSEN THE QAD RING. IF YOU DO NOT CORRECTLY HOLD THE WEIGHT (ALL OF THE TIME) AS YOU LOOSEN THE QAD RING, THE QAD RING WILL NOT OPEN. (c) Do these steps to loosen the QAD ring [24] (Figure 403). 1) Use a non-metallic hammer to hit the QAD ring [24] in the counterclockwise direction. CAUTION DO NOT TIGHTEN THE QAD BOLT TO MORE THAN 300 POUND- INCHES (34 NEWTON METERS). IF YOU TIGHTEN THE QAD BOLT TOO MUCH, YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE QAD RING. 2) Tighten the QAD bolt [22] to 300 in-lb (34 N·m). 3) Continue to hit the QAD ring [24] and tighten the QAD bolt [22] until the QAD ring [24] moves to the open position. (d) Carefully disengage the fuel pump [1] drive shaft from the Accessory Gear Box (AGB). (e) Remove the fuel pump [1] from the engine. 1) Install the cover on the AGB mounting face. (f) Remove and discard the packing support [25]. (g) Remove and discard the O-ring [26] from the fuel pump drive shaft. SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-009-F00 (11) If it is necessary, do these steps to remove the fuel pump inlet tube [12] from the fuel pump [1]. (a) Remove the four bolts [14] that hold the fuel pump inlet tube [12] and gasket [13] to the fuel pump [1]. (b) Remove the fuel pump inlet tube [12] and gasket [13]. 1) If the gasket [13] is serviceable, then keep it with the tube for the subsequent installation (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00). 2) Install protective covers on the fuel pump inlet tube [12] and fuel pump [1]. SUBTASK 73-11-11-020-010-F00 (12) If the fuel pump is replaced, remove the fuel filter differential pressure switch from the fuel pump for installation on the replacement fuel pump (TASK 73-34-01-000-801-F00). 73-11-11 SUBTASK 73-11-11-620-001-F00 (13) If it is necessary to preserve the fuel pump, do this task: Fuel Pump Preservation, TASK 73-11-01-600-801-F00.

Prepare for the Installation SUBTASK 73-11-11-840-001-F00 (1) Do these steps to prepare the installation of the parts removed from the defective fuel pump: (a) Remove the protective covers from the disconnected parts. (b) Do these steps to examine the parts: 1) Clean the mating surfaces of the parts and the adjacent areas. 2) Examine the mating surfaces of the parts and the adjacent areas. (c) Install the protective covers on the disconnected parts. (d) Examine the gaskets to make sure that they are serviceable (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00). (e) Make sure that the drain plug [2] is correctly installed. 1) If it not installed or is loose, install the drain plug [2]. WARNING DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN. a) Lubricate the new O-ring [3] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or vaseline, D00672 [CP5070]. b) Install the O-ring [3] on the drain plug [2]. c) Lubricate the threads of the drain plug [2] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or vaseline, D00672 [CP5070]. d) Install the drain plug [2]. e) Tighten the drain plug [2] to 45.0 in-lb (5.08 N·m) - 55.0 in-lb (6.21 N·m). 73-11-11 f) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the drain plug [2]. SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-001-F00 (2) If you install a replacement pump, install the fuel filter differential pressure switch that follows: (a) Do this task: Fuel Filter Differential Pressure Switch or Fuel Filter Clogging Indicator Installation, TASK 73-34-01-400-801-F00.

G. Fuel Pump Installation

SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-002-F00 CAUTION MAKE SURE THAT YOU INSTALL THE FUEL PUMP INLET-TUBE IN THE CORRECT ORIENTATION. THE FUEL SUPPLY TUBE MUST DESCRIBE AN "S" SHAPE WHEN YOU SEE IT FROM THE LEFT OF THE ENGINE. INSTALLING THE TUBE IN A DIFFERENT POSITION MAY RESULT IN CHAFING OF THE TUBE WITH THE AGB LINK AND CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE FUEL PUMP INLET TUBE. (1) Do these steps to install the fuel pump inlet tube [12] on the fuel pump [1]. (a) Lubricate these parts: 1) Lubricate the new gasket [13] with vaseline, D00672 [CP5070], or oil, D00623 [CP5066]. 2) Lubricate the threads of the bolts [14] with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101]. (b) Remove the protective covers from the bottom of the fuel pump inlet tube [12] and applicable covers from the fuel pump [1]. (c) Install the bolts [14] to connect the fuel pump inlet tube [12] and gasket [13] to the fuel pump [1]. 1) Tighten the bolts [14] to 97 in-lb (11.0 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12.4 N·m). SUBTASK 73-11-11-480-001-F00 (2) Do these steps to prepare for the installation of the fuel pump [1]. WARNING DO NOT GET FUEL IN YOUR MOUTH, OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE FUEL. KEEP THE FUEL AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . FUEL IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE, WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (a) Lubricate a new O-ring [26] with vaseline, D00672 [CP5070], or oil, D00623 [CP5066]. (b) Install the O-ring [26] in the groove of the fuel pump drive shaft. (c) Lubricate a new packing support [25] with vaseline, D00672 [CP5070], or oil, D00623 [CP5066]. (d) Install the new packing [27] on the packing support [25]. (e) Install the packing support [25] on the fixed part of the QAD ring [24]. WARNING DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN. (f) Lubricate the threads of the QAD bolt [22] with engine oil, D00599 [CP2442]. 73-11-11 SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-003-F00 (3) Do these steps to install the fuel pump [1]: (a) If it is necessary, turn the Accessory Gear Box (AGB) output shaft to align with the fuel pump drive shaft (TASK 72-63-01-000-801-F00). 1) Turn the handcranking drive pad with a 3/4-inch square drive to align the AGB drive recess with the fuel pump drive shaft splines. WARNING BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU INSTALL THE FUEL PUMP . THE FUEL PUMP WEIGHS APPROXIMATELY 46.3 POUNDS (21 KG). THE WEIGHT CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT . CAUTION DO NOT LIFT THE FUEL PUMP BY THE DRIVE SHAFT . DO NOT LET THE DRIVE SHAFT SUPPORT THE WEIGHT OF THE FUEL PUMP . IF THE DRIVE SHAFT SUPPORTS THE WEIGHT OF THE FUEL PUMP , IT CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE FUEL PUMP SEALS. (b) Engage the fuel pump [1] in the AGB. (c) Turn the QAD ring [24] clockwise until it engages with the fuel pump flange. (d) Make sure that the ball seat washer [23] is correctly installed on the QAD ring [24]. (e) Install the QAD bolt [22] and retaining packing [28] in the QAD ring [24]. CAUTION DO NOT TIGHTEN THE QAD BOLT TO MORE THAN 327 IN-LB (37 N.M). IF YOU TIGHTEN THE QAD BOLT TOO MUCH, YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE QAD RING. (f) Tighten the QAD bolt [22] to 195 in-lb (22 N·m) - 230 in-lb (26 N·m). 1) Lightly hit all around the outer surface of the QAD ring [24] with a non-metallic hammer or a rubber mallet. 2) Loosen the QAD bolt [22]. (g) Tighten the QAD bolt [22] to 292 in-lb (33 N·m) - 327 in-lb (37 N·m). 1) Lightly hit all around the outer surface of the QAD ring [24] with a non-metallic hammer or a rubber mallet. (h) Tighten the QAD bolt [22] to 292 in-lb (33 N·m) - 327 in-lb (37 N·m). (i) Measure the clearance between the QAD ring [24] and flange of the QAD ring [24] support. 1) If the clearance is more than 1.0 in. (25.4 mm) or less than 0.25 in. (6.4 mm), replace the QAD ring [24]. (j) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the QAD bolt [22]. NOTE: The final torque of the QAD bolt [22] of the QAD ring [24] will be done at the end of this procedure after the engine test. (k) Attach to the QAD bolt [22] an indicator that mentions the need to finalize the tightening procedure after the engine tests. (l) Do this task: Handcranking Drive Cover Installation, TASK 72-63-01-400-801-F00. 73-11-11 SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-004-F00 (4) Do these steps to connect the fuel supply hose [9] to the fuel pump inlet tube [12] on the fuel pump [1]. (a) Lubricate these parts: WARNING DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN. 1) Lubricate the gasket [10] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or vaseline, D00672 [CP5070]. 2) Lubricate the threads of the bolts [11] with Pure Nickel Special compound, D00006. (b) Remove the protective covers from the fuel supply hose [9] and top end of the fuel pump inlet tube [12]. (c) Put the fuel supply hose [9] and gasket [10] in the correct position on the fuel pump inlet tube [12]. NOTE: If it is not easy to put the fuel supply hose into its position, remove the bolts and clamps that hold the fuel supply hose to the fan case. (d) Use the four bolts [11] to connect the fuel supply hose [9] and gasket [10] to the fuel pump inlet tube [12]. 1) Tighten the bolts [11] to 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m) - 55 in-lb (6.2 N·m). (e) If you removed or loosened the clamps, install the clamps and bolts to connect the fuel supply hose [9] to the fan case. 1) Tighten the bolts for the clamps to 98 in-lb (11.1 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12.4 N·m). a) Make sure that there is a clearance of 0.625 in. (15.9 mm) between fuel supply hose [9] and adjacent electrical wiring. SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-005-F00 CAUTION USE TWO WRENCHES TO TIGHTEN THE TUBE COUPLING NUTS. USE ONE TO HOLD THE MATING PART , AND THE OTHER TO TIGHTEN THE COUPLING NUT . IF YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, DAMAGE TO THE TUBES AND MATING PART CAN OCCUR. (5) Do these steps to install the fuel tube [16] on the fuel pump [1] and on the hose [15]. (a) Remove the protective covers from the hose [15], fuel tube [16], and applicable covers from the fuel pump [1]. (b) Lubricate these parts: 1) Lubricate the gasket [17] with vaseline, D00672 [CP5070], or oil, D00623 [CP5066]. 2) Lubricate the threads of the bolts [18] and bolt [20] with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101]. WARNING DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN. 3) Lubricate the threads of the nipple of the fuel tube [16] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or vaseline, D00672 [CP5070]. 73-11-11 (c) Put the fuel tube [16] with the new gasket [17] in the correct position between the fuel pump [1] and hose [15]. (d) Install the four bolts [18] that hold the fuel tube [16] and gasket [17] to the fuel pump [1]. (e) Connect the fuel tube [16] to the hose [15]. (f) Use the nut [19], bolt [20], and clamp [21] to connect the fuel tube [16] to the bottom of the fuel pump [1]. (g) Make sure that the fuel tube [16] does not touch other parts of the engine. (h) Tighten the bolts [18] to 49 in-lb (5.5 N·m) - 53 in-lb (6.0 N·m). (i) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut between fuel tube [16] and hose [15] to 900 in-lb (102 N·m) - 1100 in-lb (124 N·m). (j) Tighten the nut [19] to 62 in-lb (7 N·m) - 71 in-lb (8 N·m). SUBTASK 73-11-11-410-001-F00 (6) Do this task: Main Oil/Fuel Heat Exchanger Installation, TASK 79-21-02-400-801-F00. NOTE: Do not tighten the nut [6] and bolt [7] that hold the fuel tube [8] to the bracket before the connection of the fuel tube [8] to the hose [4]. SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-006-F00 (7) Connect the fuel tube [8] to the hose [4]. CAUTION USE TWO WRENCHES TO TIGHTEN THE TUBE COUPLING NUTS. USE ONE TO HOLD THE MATING PART , AND THE OTHER TO TIGHTEN THE COUPLING NUT . IF YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, DAMAGE TO THE TUBES AND MATING PART CAN OCCUR. (a) Remove the protective covers from fuel tube [8] and hose [4]. WARNING DO NOT LET OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. YOU CAN ABSORB POISONOUS MATERIALS FROM THE OIL THROUGH YOUR SKIN. (b) Lubricate the threads of the nipple of the hose [4] with oil, D00623 [CP5066], or vaseline, D00672 [CP5070]. (c) Use your hands to connect the fuel tube [8] to the hose [4]. (d) Use two wrenches to tighten the coupling nut, between fuel tube [8] and hose [4] to a torque of 900 in-lb (102 N·m) to 1100 in-lb (124 N·m). (e) Tighten the nut [6] that holds the clamp [5] to 62 in-lb (7 N·m) - 71 in-lb (8 N·m). SUBTASK 73-11-11-410-002-F00 (8) Do this task: Servo Fuel Heater Installation, TASK 73-11-07-400-801-F00. SUBTASK 73-11-11-410-003-F00 (9) Do this task: HMU Installation, TASK 73-21-10-400-801-F00

H. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 73-11-11-840-002-F00 (2) Do these steps to put the airplane in a serviceable condition: (a) Remove the DO NOT OPERATE tag from the start lever. 73-11-11 (b) Remove the DO NOT OPERATE tag from the BAT switch, on the P5-13 panel. I. Fuel Pump Package Installation Test

SUBTASK 73-11-11-720-001-F00 CAUTION DO NOT DRY-MOTOR THE ENGINE BEFORE YOU MAKE SURE THAT THE SPAR-VALVE CLOSED LIGHT (ON THE P5 OVERHEAD PANEL) STAYS BRIGHT . IF YOU DO NOT OBEY , DAMAGE TO THE ENGINE CAN OCCUR. CAUTION MAKE SURE THAT THE APPLICABLE FOUR LOW PRESSURE LIGHTS ON THE P5 OVERHEAD PANEL ARE OFF . IF YOU DRY-MOTOR THE ENGINE AND APPLICABLE LOW PRESSURE LIGHTS ARE ON, DAMAGE TO THE ENGINE CAN OCCUR. CAUTION DO NOT DRY-MOTOR THE ENGINE BEFORE YOU MAKE SURE THAT THERE IS FUEL PRESSURE TO THE FUEL PUMP INLET . THE FUEL PUMP AND HYDRO MECHANICAL UNIT ARE FUEL LUBRICATED. ZERO FUEL PRESSURE CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE FUEL PUMP AND HYDRO MECHANICAL UNIT . J. Final Torque of the QAD Ring

SUBTASK 73-11-11-420-007-F00 CAUTION DO NOT TIGHTEN THE QAD BOLT TO MORE THAN 327 IN-LB (37 N.M). IF YOU TIGHTEN THE QAD BOLT TOO MUCH, YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE QAD RING. (1) Torque the QAD bolt [22] of the QAD ring [24]. (a) Remove safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], and indicator attached to the QAD bolt [22]. (b) Tighten the QAD bolt [22] to 292 in-lb (33 N·m) - 327 in-lb (37 N·m). (c) Loosen the QAD bolt [22]. (d) Tighten the QAD bolt [22] to 195 in-lb (22 N·m) - 230 in-lb (26 N·m). (e) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the QAD bolt [22].

G. Removal of Minor Surface Indications from the Fuel Filter Cover and Gear Housing Assembly

SUBTASK 73-11-11-320-001-F00 WARNING DO NOT BREATHE THE PARTICLES FROM BLENDING OR LET THE PARTICLES TOUCH YOU. THE PARTICLES FROM BLENDING CAN CAUSE DAMAGE, INJURY , OR IRRITATION TO YOU. USE PROTECTION EQUIPMENT . USE LOCAL MECHANICAL EXHAUST VENTILATION OR AN APPROVED RESPIRATOR. (1) Hand blend to remove the surface indications from the repair surface (TASK 70-10-07-800-801-F00), (Figure 803). (a) The table below gives dimensions for a hand blending. 73-11-11 Table 804/73-11-11-993-804-F00 Indication Depth in.(mm), Dim A Blend Depth in. (mm), Dim P=1.25Dim A Blend Length (Minimum) in. (mm) >10Dim P 0.001 in. (0.03 mm) 0.002 in. (0.05 mm) 0.02 in. (0.5 mm) 0.002 in. (0.05 mm) 0.004 in. (0.10 mm) 0.04 in. (1.0 mm) 0.004 in. (0.10 mm) 0.005 in. (0.13 mm) 0.05 in. (1.3 mm) 0.005 in. (0.13 mm) 0.006 in. (0.15 mm) 0.06 in. (1.5 mm) 0.010 in. (0.25 mm) 0.013 in. (0.33 mm) 0.13 in. (3.3 mm) 0.020 in. (0.51 mm) 0.025 in. (0.64 mm) 0.25 in. (6.4 mm) (b) Only abrasive consumables of silicon carbide grit or corundum (alumina) are permitted. (c) For hand blending use a abrasive cloth, G00363, or emery cloth, G00744, unless the steps specify differently. (d) Make sure that the Dimension P (blend depth) is approximately 1.25 times the Dimension A (indication depth). (e) Make sure that the blend length is a minimum of 10 times the Dimension P (blend depth) in all directions. 1) If it is possible, larger length-to-depth ratios are recommended. (f) Use a 10x hand held magnifying lens, STD-1070, to make sure that the blend shape is smooth, continuous, and round-bottomed, extending as far as necessary from the initially deviated area. (g) Blend the indications that have a radial direction in the same (radial) direction. (h) If the blended part involves a radius, make the radius the specified shape (Figure 803). 1) If the radius is not specified, make it a shape almost the same as the original contour. 2) If it is necessary to find the original radius, refer to the location of the part with no indication or an equivalent part. (i) It is permitted to blend sharp edges with abrasive tools. 1) Coarse grades of abrasives or files can be used for fast metal removal. 2) Make sure to give a part a smooth surface finish with fine grades of abrasives or abrasive cloth, G00363. (j) To keep abrasives working properly, do not let the abrasive become loaded up with removed material. (k) Start the blend with coarser grades of abrasive files for fast initial removal of the indication but not coarser than necessary to remove the indication. (l) Finish the blend with the finer grades of abrasive files for fast initial removal of the indication but not coarser than necessary to remove the indication. SUBTASK 73-11-11-211-002-F00 (2) Visually examine the repaired area with a 10x hand held magnifying lens, STD-1070. (a) No cracks permitted. SUBTASK 73-11-11-230-001-F00 (3) Do a fluorescent penetrant inspection, sensibility level 2, of the blended areas (TASK 70-40-01-230-801-F00 or TASK 70-40-01-910-801-F00). 73-11-11 (a) Indications less than 0.0118 in. (0.30 mm) are non-interpretable. (b) Indications more than 0.0118 in. (0.30 mm) are not permitted. (c) Linear indications are not permitted. NOTE: Linear indications are indications that are at minimum four times longer than they are wide. SUBTASK 73-11-11-300-001-F00 (4) Wet the repaired area or bare surfaces that to be touched up with de-ionized water, G02418. NOTE: Surface must stay wet between surface preparation and solution application. SUBTASK 73-11-11-300-002-F00 (5) Spot touch-up the repaired area or bare surfaces (TASK 70-10-04-380-801-F00), (Figure 801).

ATA 73 · 73-21-00

73-21-00-740-803-F00EEC BITE TEST - RECENT FAULTS ATA 73 · Task 740-803-F00

Task Description

2. EEC BITE TEST - RECENT FAULTS (Figure 501) NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. General

(1) Use the Flight Management Computer/Control Display Unit (FMCS CDU) to do the Engine EEC BITE TEST . (2) The EEC BITE - Recent Faults procedure shows maintenance messages for the three most recent flight legs. (a) The maintenance messages are stored in the EEC and show on the FMCS CDU. (b) The FMCS CDU shows the maintenance messages for only one engine at a time. (3) The FMCS CDU shows only one maintenance message on each screen. (a) The FMCS CDU shows the page you are on and the total number of pages. NOTE: If the FMCS CDU screen shows 2/4, you are on page 2 of 4 pages. (4) There are five categories of maintenance messages. The time limited dispatch limits which are given below are for on-condition events. NOTE: The CFM56-7B Engine Shop Manual (CFMI-TP .SM.10), ATA 05-17-01 is the certified authority for the Time Limited Dispatch. (a) ENGINE CONTROL LIGHT Faults - You can not dispatch the airplane with this fault. NOTE: These faults cause the ENGINE CONTROL Light to come ON. (b) ALTERNATE MODE LIGHT Fault - Refer to the Minimum Equipment List (MEL) for the dispatch limits. NOTE: These faults cause the ALTN Mode Light to come ON. 73-21-00 (c) SHORT TIME Fault - Calculate the remaining flight hours that you can operate with this fault as follows: 1) The remaining Flight Hours (R) = 150 flight hours - "Q", where "Q" is the scheduled maintenance interval your airline uses to check the EEC BITE TEST - RECENT FAULTS, SHORT TIME category. NOTE: If your airline looks for EEC faults every 70 flight hour, then "Q" = 70. If your airline looks for EEC faults every 150 flight hours, then "Q" = 150. (d) LONG TIME Fault - Calculate the remaining flight hours that you can operate with this fault as follows: 1) The remaining Flight Hours (T) = 500 flight hours - "S/2", where "S/2" is one half of the scheduled maintenance interval your airline uses to check the EEC BITE TEST - RECENT FAULTS, LONG TIME category. NOTE: If your airline looks for EEC faults every 70 flight hour, then "S/2" = 35. If your airline looks for EEC faults every 150 flight hours, then "S/2" = 75. (e) ECONOMIC Awareness Fault - There are no time limits for dispatch. Repair the problem at a convenient time. (5) The recent faults function will show the maintenance message numbers for the most recent three flight legs and one ground operation. (a) Flight Legs 1 through 3 are the three most recent flight legs. (b) Flight Leg 0: 1) Can show maintenance messages that occur more than 30 seconds after landing from the last flight leg. 2) Can show the most recent ground run of the engine. NOTE: If the engine is started and stopped more than once between flights, Flight Leg 0 will contain data from the last ground run of the engine. (c) The X below the flight leg number indicates that the fault occurred on that flight leg. 1) For flight legs that did not have the fault, the space below the flight legs number is blank.

B. References

Reference Title 24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Procedure

SUBTASK 73-21-00-740-003-F00 (1) Do these steps to get the RECENT FAULTS data for Engine 1 or Engine 2: (a) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811. (b) Get access to the FMCS CDU in the flight compartment. (c) Press the INIT REF key to show the PERF INIT screen on the FMCS CDU. NOTE: The FMCS CDU does not support a type-ahead function. You must have the prompt on the FMCS CDU screen before you type in the response. 73-21-00 (d) Push these line select keys (LSK) on the FMCS CDU: 1) INDEX 2) MAINT NOTE: This LSK causes the MAINT BITE INDEX screen to show. 3) ENGINE NOTE: This LSK causes the ENGINE/EXCEED BITE INDEX screen to show. 4) Applicable ENGINE X, (X = 1 or 2) NOTE: This LSK causes the ENGINE X BITE TEST MAIN MENU to show. Also, the ENGINE X LSK automatically applies power to the EEC and causes the EEC to initialize. The FMCS CDU will show INITIALIZING EEC X and EEC SORTING FAULT HISTORY DATA for a short time, just before the ENGINE X BITE TEST MAIN MENU shows. (e) Push the RECENT FAULTS LSK. NOTE: This LSK causes the RECENT FAULTS screen to show. The HISTORY LSK, on the Recent Faults screen, will show the fault history for the maintenance message that shows on the screen. The NEXT PAGE key will continue to show the other faults in the RECENT FAULTS format. The INDEX LSK will send you to the ENGINE X BITE TEST MAIN MENU. 1) If the FOR CH A ONLY or FOR CH B ONLY screen shows, then, do this task: CH A (B) EEC DATA NOT AVAILABLE - Fault Isolation, FIM 73-05 TASK 803. 2) Record this data from each screen: a) The Dispatch Level NOTE: The dispatch level will show at the top of the screen. The FMCS CDU will display the faults in the order of their dispatch level. The ENGINE CONTROL light faults will show first, then the ALTERNATE MODE LIGHT faults, then the SHORT TIME faults, then the LONG TIME faults, and last the ECONOMIC awareness faults. Refer to the CFM56-7B Engine Shop Manual 05-17-01, or the General Statement of this procedure for the Time Limited Dispatch capabilities for each category of message. b) Maintenance Message Number NOTE: A seven digit number with this format: AA - XDDDN. AA = ATA Chapter, X = EEC Channel (1=Channel A, 2=Channel B, 3=Channels A and B), DDD = a unique fault number, and N = Engine Position (1=Engine 1, 2=Engine 2). If the message is reported with an engine position equal to zero, then for the applicable engine, do the corrective action for Engine Position Signal is out of Range (73-X138N) (FIM 73-22 TASK 806). 3) Push the NEXT PAGE key to see the subsequent maintenance message. a) Continue to push the NEXT PAGE key until you record all of the faults. 4) If you want to go back to the previous message, push the PREV PAGE key. 5) If the ENGINE CONTROL light was ON and none of the ENGINE CONTROL light messages show during the EEC BITE Test, then, do this task: EEC TEST , TASK 73-21-00-700-804-F00. a) Look for one or more of these Maintenance Message: 73-21-00 b) 73-10201, 73-10202, 73-20201 73-20202, 73-30201, 73-30202, 73-10211, 73-10212, 73-20211 73-20212, 73-30211, 73-30212, 73-10221, 73-10222, 73-20221 73-20222, 73-30221 or 73-30222. NOTE: These INTERNAL EEC messages can set the ENGINE CONTROL light, but the problem that causes the fault also causes problems with the EEC BITE Test. When this occurs, the EEC cannot write to the EEC fault memory. c) Do the corrective action in the FIM for the messages that you find. (f) If the fault data is not available from one of the two channels (A and B) of the EEC, the screen will show the EEC channel that has data. 1) Example: FOR CH B (A) ONLY , CH A (B) EEC DATA NOT AVAILABLE, CAN NOT ACCESS CH A (B) (g) If you are in RECENT FAULTS and there are no faults stored for the flight legs 0 through 3, the screen will show NO RECENT FAULTS STORED. (h) If you wish to do other tests, push the INDEX LSK several times, until the correct menu shows. (i) To end the test, push the INIT REF key. NOTE: This causes the test to stop and automatically removes electrical power from the EEC.

Task Description

A. General

(b) The FMCS CDU shows the maintenance messages for only one engine at a time. (3) The FMCS CDU shows only one maintenance message on each screen. (a) The FMCS CDU shows the page you are on and the total number of pages. NOTE: If the FMCS CDU screen shows 2/4, you are on page 2 of 4 pages. (4) There are five categories of maintenance messages. The time limited dispatch limits which are given below are for on-condition events. NOTE: The CFM56-7B Engine Shop Manual (CFMI-TP .SM.10), ATA 05-17-01 is the certified authority for the Time Limited Dispatch. (a) ENGINE CONTROL LIGHT Faults - You can not dispatch the airplane with this fault. NOTE: These faults cause the ENGINE CONTROL Light to come ON. (b) ALTERNATE MODE LIGHT Fault - Refer to the Minimum Equipment List (MEL) for the dispatch limits. NOTE: These faults cause the ALTN Mode Light to come ON. (c) SHORT TIME Fault - Calculate the remaining flight hours that you can operate with this fault as follows: 1) The remaining Flight Hours (R) = 150 flight hours - "Q", where "Q" is the scheduled maintenance interval your airline uses to check the EEC BITE TEST - RECENT FAULTS, SHORT TIME category. NOTE: If your airline looks for EEC faults every 70 flight hour, then "Q" = 70. If your airline looks for EEC faults every 150 flight hours, then "Q" = 150. (e) ECONOMIC Awareness Fault - There are no time limits for dispatch. Repair the problem at a convenient time. (5) The fault history function will show the maintenance message numbers for the ten most recent flight legs and one ground operation. (a) Flight Leg 1 shows the data for the most recent flight leg. (b) Flight Leg 10 shows the data for the oldest flight leg that is stored in the system. (c) Flight Leg 0: 73-21-00 1) Can show maintenance messages that occur more than 30 seconds after landing from the last flight leg. 2) Can show the most recent ground run of the engine. NOTE: If the engine is started and stopped more than once between flights, Flight Leg 0 will contain data from the last ground run of the engine. (6) The X below the flight leg number indicates that the fault occurred on that flight leg. (a) For flight legs that did not have the fault, the space below the flight legs number is blank.

73-21-00-840-801-F00Prepare for the Tests ATA 73 · Task 840-801-F00

Task Description

4. Prepare for the Tests

A. References

Reference Title 24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201)

B. Location Zones

5. FADEC System Test (Figure 501)

A. General

(1) This task is the complete test of the FADEC (Full Authority Digital Engine Controller) system. (2) The task is used when the EEC or the Engine is replaced.

B. References

Reference Title 71-00-00-700-807-F00 Test 12 - Actuators Test (P/B 501) 73-21-60-470-801-F00 EEC Software Load (P/B 201)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right 73-21-00

D. FADEC System Test

SUBTASK 73-21-00-700-002-F00 (1) When you do the FADEC system test, you must make sure the configuration of the engine and EEC is compatible with the opposite engine and EEC, the airplane and thrust reversers. NOTE: Refer to Boeing SB 737-71-1588 for concurrent requirements necessary for installation of a T ech Insertion (TI) CFM56-7BXX/3 engine or CFM56-7BXX SAC engine incorporating CFM-SB 72-0583 on 737-600/700/800/900/BBJ airplanes from Line Positions 1 through 2229. NOTE: Refer to Boeing SB 737-71-1551 for concurrent requirements necessary for installation of TI CFM56-7BXX/3 engine or CFM56-7BXX SAC engine incorporating CFM-SB 72-0583 on 737-700/800 airplanes from Line Positions 2230 through 2252. NOTE: Refer to Boeing SB 737-71-1582 for concurrent requirements necessary for installation of non-TI engine (CFM56-7BXX) engine or CFM56-7BXX SAC engines incorporating CFM-SB 72-0583 on airplanes delivered with TI engines (CFM56-7BXX/3) on 737-600/700/800/900/BBJ airplanes from Line Positions 2253 and on and all 737-900ER airplanes. NOTE: A T ech Insertion (TI) engine has a “/3” after the thrust rating on the engine data plate. An example of a Singular Annular Combustor (SAC) Engine with TI is CFM56-7B22/3. An example of a non-TI engine is CFM56-7B22. The “XX” in CFM56-7BXX/3 is the thrust rating of the engine. (a) Make sure the EEC software version on each engine is compatible with the other engine, thrust reverser and airplane. Refer to the task EEC Software Load, TASK 73-21-60-470-801-F00. 1) Intermix of some EEC software versions is not permitted. 2) Some EEC software versions must be installed on both engines at the same time. 3) Some EEC software versions require concurrent changes to the airplane configuration. SUBTASK 73-21-00-740-004-F00 (2) For an EEC replacement, do this task: Erase All EEC Faults, TASK 73-21-00-800-801-F00 SUBTASK 73-21-00-730-001-F00 (3) Do these steps to do the FADEC System Test: (a) Do this task: Prepare for the Tests, TASK 73-21-00-840-801-F00.
